# Handoff log

Append-only. One entry per session. A new session reads the last entry first.

## Handoff 012 — 2026-11-04
**State:** `in_023` on `cad-ui` (the human sent a low-angle screenshot): at low angles the plane looked like it
separated and faded away instead of the camera passing through it. Cause: a grazing-angle alpha fade in the plane
shader (added early against shimmer; redundant since grid levels fade by on-screen cell size) plus a fog that

## Handoff 016 — 2026-11-04
**State:** Bug report `in_029`: after clicking a sheet the human got "locked" on it and could not get back to the 3D view,
which also blocked tuning the sheet look. Likely causes, all fixed in `prototypes/cad-ui`:
1. Esc dropped the sketch plane but left the camera face-on, so nothing visibly changed. Now Esc (from the select tool), the
   menu entry "Back to 3D view" and a new **3D view** button in the status line glide the camera back to the pose it
   came from (or the default oblique view if that was already face-on).
2. The status line had lost its exit hint in stage 2; the button restores it.
3. Orbit had moved to right-drag only. Plain left-drag now orbits when the left button has nothing else to do (3D view; select
   tool on empty space), and Alt + left-drag orbits (Shift+Alt+left-drag pans), so a trackpad or a mouse without a usable
   right button is never trapped.
4. On the tuning page, keys were ignored while a panel slider had focus; sliders, colors and buttons now release the keyboard.
The tuning panel gained its own buttons (3D view, Sketch XY/XZ/YZ, Pin hover sheet, Test boxes) so the look can be tuned
without mouse navigation. 21 unit tests plus scripted browser runs (the lock-in scenarios: 12 checks, and the earlier stage 2
workflow: 27 checks) all pass. `dec_ui_conventions` records the correction.

**Open for the human:** do the new exits feel right (Esc glide, the button, left-drag orbit)? Then tune the sheet look with
`bash tools/preview.sh tune` and send the values.
**Next:** stage 3 (extrude with Shapr3D-style defaults, history list, drawer).

## Handoff 017 — 2026-11-04
**State:** `in_030`: the human could not tune sheet brightness separately from transparency, and the fog was only on the sheet,
not behind it. Rebuilt the sheet as three passes in `prototypes/cad-ui`: a paint layer (**opacity**, sheet color), a glow layer
(**brightness**, screen-blended so it does not clip what is behind it to white; my first additive version made a bright sheet
turn a box behind it pure white, which was caught by looking at the screenshot), and the grid and axis lines. The fog is split in two:
**sheetArea** (backlight area: how far a sheet's light, grid and axes reach; carries the values the human tuned) and **fog** (world
fog: a real scene fog on objects, 0.9 to 3.5 view distances). The tuning panel now has Brightness, Opacity, Backlight area and
World fog sections, plus a "Pinned look" toggle so the selected look can be viewed in the 3D view with test boxes. Verified with
screenshots (bright and see-through; bright and veiled; objects fading in fog), 20 unit tests, and the lock-in (12) and stage 2 (27)
scripted checks, all passing.

**Open for the human:** tune brightness and opacity (selected and hovered) and the two falloffs with `bash tools/preview.sh tune`
and send the values.
**Next:** apply the human's values and remove the panel; then stage 3 (extrude).

## Handoff 018 — 2026-11-04
**State:** `in_031` (with a screenshot): the human tuned the look and sent the values (applied verbatim; 37 values checked: world #242424,
sheet #f3f0e7, brightness 1.2, opacity 0.5, grid line 0.5, fine 0.5 for both selected and hovered; world fog 0.5 to 2.5; everything else
unchanged), asked for slightly reflective sheets, and reported getting the view "covered in fog" after zooming and panning.
- **Lost origin.** Reproduced: zoom-to-cursor and panning moved the target 430 mm from the origin in a few moves, far past where the axes fog
  out. Root cause beyond plain panning: zooming OUT anchored on the cursor point multiplies the offset (a 50x zoom-out moved the target ~50x
  away). Fixed: zoom in toward the cursor, zoom out about the view center; anchor only on plane points within 1.5 view-distances of the
  target; a small "Origin is far away: return (O)" button appears when the origin is lost (fog strength below 0.3, or off screen and over
  1 view-distance away); **O** and a "Return to origin" menu item glide the target back to the origin keeping the viewing direction (so a sketch
  stays face-on) and clamp the distance to 30..600 mm. 12 scripted checks pass.
- **Reflective sheets.** A sky sheen (neutral light-above, dark-below gradient reflected by the view vector) plus a real mirror image of
  objects (mirrored camera into a half-resolution render target, clipped to the camera side of the plane; reflectables on layer 1, lights too),
  both Fresnel-weighted; premultiplied blending so reflected objects blend toward their color. Defaults sheen 0.22, objects 0.35. The tuning
  panel has a Reflection section (strengths, straight-on reflectivity, glancing sharpness, sky colors). Nothing real is reflected yet except
  the test boxes; extruded bodies will be. Two bugs found by looking at screenshots: lights on the wrong layer rendered reflected objects
  black, and screen-blending a dark reflection onto a bright sheet was almost invisible.
- 20 unit tests plus scripted browser suites (lock-in 12, stage 2 27, origin 12) all pass; no console errors.

**Open for the human:** tune the reflection values with `bash tools/preview.sh tune` (sheen, object reflection, glancing sharpness); try the
lost-origin prompt and O.
**Next:** stage 3 (extrude with Shapr3D-style defaults, history list, drawer); reflections will then show real bodies.

Dragging it lifts the figure out of the scene (the preview stands in) and makes it again where you let go: the figure stays one history step. Typing a size while it is up makes it again at that size (Tab for the second field). A click anywhere else, Enter or Esc ends the drawing and the figure stays. The keys R C L and the menu start the same thing.
**The pencil:** a selected plane or face (body face, drawn figure, or a world plane chosen with the X Y Z buttons) shows a pencil bubble at its middle; clicking it replaces it with rectangle, circle and line bubbles; choosing one arms the tool on that plane. Esc closes the choice. (`modesFor`, `AppState.sketching`.)
**Removed:** the second click that finished a figure, and the line that chains corners and closes onto the first: a line is one segment now.
**Not done / for you to say:** chained lines and a closed polygon (a pencil shape for it?); a world plane cannot be selected by a click so the pencil there needs the X Y Z button first; the ball is only for the first figure, afterwards select the figure and use its constraints; default size 10.

## Handoff 114 — 2026-11-05 (the shake: the kernel thread's stale answers)
**State:** `in_134`. 178 tests pass, checks clean.
**Tried it:** in a real browser I dragged the Move arrow and a Rotate ring on a box with all its edges filleted, sampling the body and the handle every frame. On the plain box it was smooth; on the filleted one the body jumped back to its starting place every few frames while the arrow kept going: that is the shake you saw.
**Cause (mine, from the kernel thread of Handoff 112):** while the worker is still busy, the kernel call answers with "the last result". Reading a result that was already known (the committed model, for example) also made it "the last result", so the next unanswered frame showed the body back where it started. **Fix:** "the last result" is only what the worker finished last, never one read from the cache (`kernel-async.ts`). After the fix the body follows the handle without jumping back; on a heavier model it trails the handle by about a frame, since the kernel answers a moment after the question (it never freezes the page now). A test with a stand-in worker (`test/kernel-async.test.ts`) covers the rule.
**Not fixed, to know:** on a much heavier model the body trails the arrow more (the arrow follows the pointer at once, the body when the kernel has answered). If that is too much I can draw only the arrow's outline until the answer comes, or fall back to running the kernel in the page for light models; tell me what you see.

## Handoff 118 — 2026-11-06 (Ø and R, the lag, the extrude that did nothing)
**State:** `in_138`. 194 tests pass, checks clean. Browser: the model opens with its two new pieces (volume 3297, was 2050).
**Diameter or radius?** The drawing standard: **Ø (diameter)** for a full circle, a round hole or a post (that is what is dimensioned, and what you measure with a caliper); **R (radius)** for a part of a circle, an arc or a fillet. So: a circle's diameter bubble shows Ø; a full round post or hole, when clicked, shows its push / pull bubble as Ø and typing sets the diameter; a fillet or other partly round face of a cylinder or sphere shows R and its radius (read only) on the face. The mark is in front of the number (`Annotation.symbol`, `short` in the value bubble).
**Why nothing was extruded:** a figure pulled the negative way while a body exists was always a cut; drawn in the open (a world plane, away from the body) it cut nothing, so nothing appeared. It is now "cut or add": if the pulled-back figure meets the body and removes material it is a pocket (a figure drawn on a face and pushed in is as before), otherwise it is a new piece (`mode: cutOrAdd` in the kernel). Both pieces of your model now appear. All the pieces are still one 'Body 1' in the list (a body here is the whole result): separate bodies you can select one by one are not built.
**The lag:** the kernel worked out the whole model again for every frame of the drag (about 250 ms for your model). It now keeps what it worked out before, by what each step was built from, so a move at the end redoes only the move: about 90 ms (opening your model went from 4.0 s to 1.3 s). What is left is mostly making the picture (faces, edges, mesh) of the result; if it is still not smooth enough, the next step is a coarser mesh while dragging.
**Not done:** several bodies as separate selectable things; a coarser mesh while dragging.

**Tangent:** select a line and a circle, or a straight line and an arc: `Tangent` (the line's distance from the centre equals the radius). A circle or arc keeps its size and place softly while the line moves to touch it.
**Not done:** tangent between two arcs or circles; an arc by clicking the centre and then the radius (the centre is made for you); a tangent that also joins the line to the arc's end (do both: coincident then tangent); the arc has no length-along-the-curve dimension (its length is the chord's).

## Handoff 121 — 2026-11-06 (repeat edit mode: Apply / Cancel, bubbles per axis, the pivot as a vertex)
**State:** `in_141`. 201 tests pass, checks clean. Browser: a linear repeat shows ✓ and ✕ at the top, a count / distance bubble on each axis; a circular one shows the rings at the pivot, a line from the body's middle to it, and one bubble (count, degrees) to the side.
**Repeat edit mode (`AppState.repeat`):** Linear repeat, Circular repeat and Copy open a repeat that is its own state, not an operation. It ends only with ✓ Apply or ✕ Cancel (top of the screen); a click on nothing, Esc, Enter in a bubble, or dragging a handle do not end it. The result shows as you go. Meanwhile you can select, draw (rectangle, circle, line, curve), Move and Rotate (right-click ring) and use constraints. Not offered meanwhile (dimmed with the reason): push / pull, fillet / chamfer, scale, copy and the repeats again, delete; undo and redo are ignored (they could take the pivot's parts away).
**Linear:** three bubbles, one at the end of each axis's spacing arrow: `X×` how many, `Δ` how far apart (same for Y and Z). The count arrows (short ones past the last copy) now start 0.9 of a handle length beyond the last copy while there is only one (clear of the spacing arrows), and 0.35 once there are copies.
**Circular:** the three rings only; **a click on a ring makes it the axis** (dragging it also sets the degrees); one bubble `n` (copies) and `°` (degrees), to the right of the rings so it does not cover them.
**The pivot:** when a circular repeat opens, two vertices and a line are added to the scene: one at the middle of what is repeated (held fixed) and the pivot point, with the line between them. They are ordinary drawn parts: select the line to size it (its length bubble), select the pivot to constrain it (coincident, on a line, ...), or Move it with the ring. The repeat follows wherever the pivot is. For a drawn figure they lie on its plane; for a body on a plane of their own through the pivot. Apply or Cancel takes them away (the copies, if applied, are made first, in one undo step; the adding of the parts when it opened is a step of its own in the history).
**Not done / know:** the rotation axis passes through the pivot parallel to X, Y or Z (not along a drawn line); the middle vertex stays where the object's middle was when the repeat opened (if you move the object meanwhile it does not follow); a repeat is not saved in a file while it is open.

## Handoff 122 — 2026-11-06 (the hole that broke: steps made on an older picture, and steps that could not be made)
**State:** `in_142`. 204 tests pass, checks clean. Your file is a test fixture; opened, it shows the three broken steps in red.
**What happened in your file:** the last four push / pulls of the hole (s_024 to s_027) all name the hole as it was before the first of them (radius 2.2405), and s_025 asks to take 2.7 mm off a hole that is 2.24 mm in radius: that cannot be made ("the radius must be positive"), so s_025 failed and s_026 and s_027, built on it, failed with it: the model stopped at s_024 and the picture was stale and odd. Two faults of mine made that possible: (1) with the kernel on its own thread, the picture can show the model from before your last change for a moment, and a step started then names the old hole (it was made from an older picture); (2) a step that the kernel could not make was kept in the history anyway.
**Fixes:** (1) while the kernel's answer for the last change is still to come, a new step is refused ("the model is still being worked out: try again in a moment"); what is shown meanwhile is marked as old (`KernelResult.stale`). (2) A step that cannot be made is refused at once, with the reason, and nothing is added (`session.run`); if the answer comes later (kernel on its thread) and it is an error, the step is taken back and the reason shown (`refresh`, `checkSteps`). (3) A push / pull of a round face whose size is not what the step remembers (an earlier step changed it) now finds the same hole by its axis, place and nearest size instead of failing. (4) The history list shows a step that could not be made in red with ✕ and the reason on hover; × takes it out. (5) A job the worker fails on gives an answer (an error), so nothing waits for it for ever.
**Repairing your file:** open it, History (⋯), the last three steps are red: take out the three (s_027, s_026, s_025 with ×), or just the first and the others go with it as they need it. Opening a file with broken steps does not change it.
**Know:** steps already in a file are not changed; the typed size of a hole is stored as a distance from the size it had, not as the size you typed (so a step is only right for the hole as it was).

## Handoff 138 — 2026-11-06 (bug hunt: a freeze in the solver, cycling selection, bubble cost)
**State:** `in_158`. 217 tests pass, checks clean.
**How I hunted:** two random-action fuzzers (kept out of the repo, in the session scratch): one drives the controller with random clicks, hovers, tools, typing, undo, ring actions and times each step and each frame (thousands of steps, many seeds); one does the same in the real page through `window.__cad`. Nothing threw. They found a freeze and a slowness:
**1. The freeze (most likely yours):** adding a constraint (here Horizontal on one line) to a drawing of ~120 parts took **15 seconds** on the page's thread. The constraint solver is dense (cost grows with the cube of the numbers in the system) and, when a constraint conflicts, it tries leaving out each of up to 80 constraints in turn, each a full solve. Fixes (`compiler/solver/solver.mjs`): (a) constraints that share no corner or number are solved as separate groups (a drawing of many separate shapes costs the sum, not the cube); (b) the "which constraint breaks the others" search stops after 0.4 s and names what it found. The same runs went from 15 s / 2.8 s / 1.9 s to under 1 s. Tests added. Know: one big connected sketch with a conflict can still take about a second; the solver should eventually move off the page's thread or go sparse.
**2. Circle edge hard to pick (second cause found):** at a spot where a corner, a line and the circle overlap, repeated clicks are meant to cycle through them ("2 of 4 here"), but the spot was recognised by the candidates *in order of distance*, and a pixel of movement changes that order, so the cycle restarted at the first (the corner) every time and the circle was never reached. Now the same set of candidates is the same spot whatever the order, and the cycle keeps the order of the first click (`interaction/selection.ts`, test added). With the earlier fixes (double / triple click no longer swallow overlapping edges) a single click within 6 px of a circle's ring always selects it (checked at every 5° round it, seen from above and from the home view). Still there by design: the click right after drawing a line on its vertex starts a new line (`in_151`); Escape first.
**3. Page cost:** the bubbles' dodging was done by sampling along every line for every candidate spot; now it clips each line against the candidate's box and skips lines nowhere near, and the list of what to avoid is not even built when no bubble is shown.
**Seen, not fixed:** in the headless browser a page with ~70 drawn parts takes 0.3 s a frame, nearly all of it in the browser's own drawing (software rendering here); I could not tell how much of that a real graphics card would show. If it freezes again, tell me roughly how much was drawn and what you did last.

## Handoff 145 — 2026-11-06 (finer mesh for small parts; a live ghost while dragging a round face; a plan for detail levels)
**State:** `in_165`. 225 tests pass, checks clean.
**Answer to "is it the graphics?":** mostly no. There were two separate causes. (1) The mesh's fineness was one fixed value for every part (deflection 0.05 mm, 17°), so a 2 mm post was a ~20-sided prism: that is why small diameters looked worst. (2) Each size is a new kernel build (30 to 120 ms), so the body updates in jumps of about 10 to 30 a second however fast the graphics are. Drawing the body costs almost nothing next to that.
**Done now:** (1) the kernel meshes each part finer the smaller it is: deflection is 0.2 % of the part's size (never coarser than 0.05 mm, never finer than 0.002 mm), 11° angular (`occt/evaluate.mjs`): a 4 mm post is 150+ triangles. (2) While the arrow of a round face is dragged, the size the pointer is at right now is drawn at once as thin teal rings at the two ends and a dozen lines between them (`frame.withGhost`), at the pointer's rate; the body catches up behind it.
**The idea you suggested** (simplify in motion, refine when stopped) is written up as `q_motion_detail` (open): three levels (moving: local cheap ghost; just stopped: the kernel's body; settled: one finer mesh swapped in), with what is built (the mesh-by-size, the ghost for round faces) and what is not (the settled refinement; ghosts for the other operations; adapting to the device). Tell me which to build.
**Not tried:** the ghost on a phone; whether the finer mesh slows a big model's rebuild (it only gets finer for small parts, the big ones are as before).

## Handoff 156 — 2026-11-06 (playing with the face editing: what I found and fixed)
**State:** `in_175`. 239 tests pass, checks clean.
**How:** your model opened and re-played step by step (it is sound: the final body has volume 1055, no errors; the edits you made, changing a step's depth, moving a sketch's side, resizing a radius, are all in it); a sweep that pulls and pushes every face of a model by +1, −1 and +4 mm (typed as the size) and records which way it was done, the volume change, steps added, messages and errors, on your model, `channel-block`, `hole-edits` and `cyl-fillet`; real-mouse runs in the page on your model; my own: boxes, a hexagon, cylinders with chamfers, holes.
**Found and fixed:**
1. **Most edits were refused** and fell back to a prism, because a later step finds its face by the plane it was on, and the edit had moved that plane. A step now finds the face again as the nearest one facing the same way, under the point it was found by, if clearly the nearest (`findFace` in the kernel, as for edges). On your model the number of faces that could be edited by their step went from about half to most.
2. **A fillet face that could not take a size** said "face not found" (it tried a prism, which does not exist for a fillet). Fillet and chamfer faces have no prism fall-back now; the message says the size cannot be made and why.
3. **A size that is nothing or less** (a fillet bigger than the edge allows, a radius below zero) did nothing without a word: it says "that size is not possible: it would be zero or less".
4. **The side of a hexagon** (any shape whose neighbours are not square to the side) was moved straight out, which tilted its neighbours. Now the neighbouring sides are extended or cut to meet the moved side, so a hexagon stays a hexagon (its side gets shorter as it moves out, since the neighbours lean towards each other: that is the real geometry). Rectangles are as before. Tests added.
**Still refused and made as a prism, seen in the sweep:** some sides and ends where a later step names an edge that cannot be found again after the change (two similar edges, so not clearly nearest); a chamfer or fillet that would be too big for the edge it is on. The message says so; the prism is the old behaviour.
**Not a bug, but you should know:** clicking the middle of a face in the page sometimes picks one of its corner points or edges instead (a vertex is picked from 10 px away, a face is only picked away from them), and a drag then starts a fillet / chamfer of that edge: that is how points and edges win over faces.

## Handoff 157 — 2026-11-06 (fillets at a corner; one arrow for several edges)

**The inner fillet, and the answer to "too deep into OCCT?":** it is not too deep: the kernel *can* round a corner where several edges meet, but only when those edges are rounded **in the same operation**; one at a time, the second or third edge at a corner is often refused ("the kernel could not make a fillet of that size…"), even with a small size. Also, a fillet whose edge cannot be found by name any more (the earlier fillets moved its ends) is found as the nearest edge, on the body before. Tested: three fillets in turn at one corner of a block (the third used to fail); three edges at one corner selected together also work (they always did).

**What you asked:** a circle with two radii drawn to points on it should give a quarter and a three-quarter piece, each selectable and extrudable.
**Now:** lines whose ends are both on a circle (a point with the on-circle constraint) or at its centre cut the circle: the circle is no longer one disk; its pieces are faces of their own (the faces of the drawing made of those lines and the arcs of the circle between the points where they meet it, found by walking round the drawing). Radii, chords, or both together work; a line that leads nowhere (a single radius) cuts nothing, and then the circle is a disk as before. The arcs are derived parts (`Line.derived`, counter-clockwise arcs about the circle's centre; never saved, not picked, rebuilt with the faces), so a piece is a loop of ordinary parts and an extrude step names them like any other. A piece that shares a side with an extruded piece is not used up with it: the shared sides show again for it. Test: your file, both pieces extruded one after the other add up to the whole cylinder; each alone is a quarter / three quarters of it.
**Also:** arcs in a figure are now 64 steps (was 24) in the outline and in the extruded shape, since a three-quarter piece looked faceted.
**Not done / know:** pieces are extruded as polygons of the arc points (as every arc is, today), so their curved wall is facets, not a true cylinder wall, and push/pull of that wall makes a prism; the whole disk of a cut circle can no longer be extruded in one go (extrude its pieces together, select them both); a line between a point on the circle and one not on it, or a cut by lines that cross inside the circle, is not handled; I did not drive this in a real browser run, only in tests.

## Handoff 163 — 2026-11-06 (general split into regions)
**State:** `in_183`. 255 tests pass, checks clean. New: `src/operation/regions.ts` (replaces the circle-only cutting of Handoff 161).
**What it does:** lines, arcs and circles on one plane that cross, touch or end on each other cut the plane into regions, each a face of its own: a rectangle with a diagonal is two triangles; lines across a rectangle or circle cut it up (the part of a line outside a circle cuts nothing and is left alone); two overlapping circles are the lens and two crescents; a line from a point on a circle to a free point inside it cuts nothing (the circle stays a whole disk); radii and chords as before. How: every curve is split at its crossings and at the points where an end of another curve lies on it; the faces are found by walking round the pieces (face kept on the left, outside turns the other way and is dropped); pieces that lead nowhere are dropped first. A closed shape that nothing touches is still made the plain way (loop of lines, disk), so nothing drawn before changes.
**Parts:** the pieces and the crossing points are derived parts (`derived` lines and corners; never saved, not picked), named from the curves they come from (`d/seg@…`, `d/arc@…`, `d/x@…`) so an extrude step that names them still finds them while the drawing keeps its shape; a piece that is a whole original line or arc keeps its own name. An extruded region does not use up a side it shares with a region that was not extruded.
**Know / not done:** crossing points are new points that follow the curves, but nothing is constrained to them (no snapping to them or constraints on them yet; they are not picked); arcs drawn as a chain with a "bulge" (not the arc tool) are not cut; collinear overlapping lines are not split; two curves tangent to each other are barely handled; points closer than 0.0001 mm merge; if you move a line so that two crossings swap order the names stay but the shape is whatever it now is; not driven in a real browser run (tests only: diagonal, grid, line across a circle, overlapping circles, extruding regions one after another).

## Handoff 166 — 2026-11-07 (rims that grow past each other; the chamfer's lower edge; double click on the body)
**State:** `in_186`. 262 tests pass, checks clean. No model file came with this message, so I rebuilt the case: a ring (outer 20, bore 10), a chamfer on both top rims, then the outer diameter grown.
**1. The bore went wrong when the outer diameter grew (found, fixed).** A fillet or chamfer finds its edge again after a size change by "the nearest edge, if clearly the nearest". The outer rim grown from 20 towards 30 is as near the bore's old 10 as to its own old 20 (a tie: the step failed with "line not found"), and beyond 30 the bore's rim is the nearer one: the chamfer jumped to the bore and made it bigger (your symptom). A round rim now carries its place in the row of rims that share a centre and an axis, smallest first (`k/n`, step param `ranks`, made when the fillet / chamfer is started: `operation/rims.ts`), and the kernel finds the k-th smallest of the rims at that centre now (`edgeByRank`), however much they grew. Steps made before this have no ranks and still use "nearest". Tested: outer 25, 30, 40, 60 with both chamfers kept (volumes checked).
**2. The chamfer's lower edge inside a bore could not be picked (found, fixed).** An edge between two curved faces (the cone and the bore wall) was taken for "hidden behind a face" because the mesh of a curved face lies a hair inside the true surface; the hidden-behind test was too tight (1 mm part in a million). It is 1.5 mm per metre of distance now. On the far side of the bore the lower chamfer edge is picked in the browser run and in a test (the near side really is hidden behind the ring).
**3. The flat disc inward (not reproduced).** Shrinking a bore by pulling its wall changes the bore circle's radius and the chamfer moves with it (lower edge and top rim both kept; tested on the ring); I could not make it add a flat disc. That needs your file: the step list on your model would show whether the inner ring came from a pushed face (a prism step) instead of a changed radius.
**4. Double click on any part of the body.** A double click on a point or line of the body (when only body parts are under the pointer) takes the whole body, like on a face; a third click selects only that part. Where a drawn thing lies on the same spot (a drawn corner on a body corner) repeated clicks still cycle (the old test). Slow repeated clicks still cycle through what is under the pointer.

## Handoff 167 — 2026-11-07 (audit after the bore bug)
**State:** `in_188`. 268 tests pass, checks clean. New tests in `test/audit.test.ts`.
**Found and fixed:**
1. **A push / pull step on a round wall could jump to the wrong wall (silent).** A ring's outer wall pushed (a step on the wall), then the outer circle made bigger: the step found the bore as the "nearest radius" and made the bore smaller. A wall now has to be the same kind (post or hole) as the step remembers, and if two walls are about equally near it reports "not found" instead of choosing.
2. **A fillet or chamfer on a straight edge failed when the block was widened (about 1.5 times or more), a very common thing to do.** The edge was found by nearness, which fails when the edge itself moves far. A straight edge now also remembers the two flat faces it lies between (`edgeSignatures`, step param `sigs`, made when the fillet is started) and is found again as the edge between those two faces (and in the same direction), which is the same edge however big the block becomes. Tested with widths 30, 60, 100.
3. **A line started from a crossing point of two curves was attached to the derived crossing point,** which is rebuilt from the drawing and goes when the crossing goes (the line would lose its end). It now starts on a real point.
4. **Deleting one piece of a cut shape took the circle and both cut lines with it.** It now takes only the sides nothing else borders (and their free points); where that is nothing (a piece of a cut circle: its radii are shared and the arc is the circle's) there is no Delete.
5. **Move, turn, scale, repeat and offset of a single piece of a cut shape did garbage or gave a message about "a reference".** They now say why not (it shares its sides with the other pieces); extruding it is as before.
**Checked and fine:** the kernel's cache key covers every op that names another (holes, siblings, the earlier body of a together-chain); the circle cutting held its tolerance through radius changes from 0.5 to 80 on your pie file (points stay within 1e-9 of the circle).
**Not done / know:** flat faces found again by nearness (`faceExtrude` steps on a face) were not probed beyond the existing tests: they look "under the point" so are safer but could still jump between stacked faces of the same facing; edges that are not straight and not circles (ellipses, splines) have no signature; edge signatures need the edge to lie between two flat faces (an edge between a flat and a curved face still uses nearness); steps made before this change have no `ranks` / `sigs` and keep the nearness rule; command time with many curves (about 0.1 s at 40) was not improved; touch gestures were not part of this audit.

## Handoff 176 — 2026-11-07 (the belt: lines tangent to circles at their ends)
**State:** `in_197`. 288 tests pass, checks clean. Your file is `app/test/fixtures/belt.cad.json`.
**Yes, it is closed, and it could not be extruded because no regions were found at all (not even the two disks).** Each line leaves its circle exactly along the circle's tangent, so at the point where they meet, the line and the arc leave in the very same direction. My region finder orders the pieces round a point by the direction they leave in, and with two leaving the same way the order was arbitrary, so every walk round a region went wrong and was thrown away. It now tells them apart by which way they curve as they leave (a line straight, an arc turning left or right with 1/radius), the same way a person would: the one that curves further left comes later round the point. On your file there are three regions: the two disks and the belt between the lines (volumes checked when extruded 5 mm: 677, 203 and 373 mm³).
**Know / not done:** pieces that leave a point the same way *and* curve the same way (two equal circles touching along a tangent point with the same radius on the same side: not possible) are not told apart; a drawing made with the trim tool is now closed up as you would expect (this file came from it).

## Handoff 179 — 2026-11-07 (the zoom, and a state file to share)
**State:** `in_200`. 290 tests pass, checks clean.
**The zoom: what I found by playing with it (a long run of wheel clicks at random places, with and without a body, in and out).**
1. **Zooming out was not the opposite of zooming in (the main cause).** Zooming in toward a surface under the pointer multiplies the distance *to that surface* by the factor (and slides the view's centre toward it), but zooming out multiplied the distance *to the view's centre*, which after zooming in on a surface is much shorter than the surface's. Zooming in 25 clicks on a box's top and out 25 clicks took the view to 668 mm from a start of 120 mm (5.6 times farther than it began). Going in and out at distant places (your "distant pane areas") made the view end up anywhere. Zooming out is now anchored on the surface under the pointer in the same way (and about the middle when there is none), so the same clicks back at the same place return the view exactly (120.00 → in → out → 120.00, the target back at the origin). The one place it cannot be exact: going in stops when the surface is as near as the view may get; the clicks out do not stop.
2. **The floor (the world plane that is on) counted as a surface to zoom toward**, and it is under every pointer, so each click in walked the view's centre across the floor, further the shallower the view: that is the wander toward "the fog" (since the floor was made everywhere, `in_190`). It is no longer a surface to zoom toward; only the body and your drawings are. (Zooming in on empty floor zooms about the middle of the view.)
Tests: a zoom in and out at the same place is the inverse, the floor does not pull the view. The old test that said "zoom out is about the middle" was changed to say this.
**Not found:** anything else that made the zoom odd in my runs (no NaN, no distance stuck at the minimum, nothing runaway). If it still goes strange, use the new file below right when it does.
**Share state (new).** ⋯ menu, "Share state (a file to send for a bug report)": downloads `cad-state-<date-time>.json` with: the whole project (it is a normal project file inside the `project` key: open it, or ask me to), the camera (target, distance, angles, straight or not, the mix of perspective, where the eye is), the selection and what is hovered and what is under the pointer, the tool and operation state, the kernel's errors / volume / size, the parts by kind and the steps, the window and device, the build and your settings for the view, and the last 400 things you did (each wheel click, drag, click, key, with the camera after each move of the view; hovering is only its last position). Send me the file; `window.__cad.snapshot()` gives the same in the console. `interaction/snapshot.ts`, test: the snapshot is JSON and its project opens again.
**Know:** the file holds your whole model, so look before sharing if that matters; it is not sent anywhere by the page.

## Handoff 182 — 2026-11-07 (constraints stay; scale carries the sketch)
**State:** `in_205`. 294 tests pass; the whole of `npm run check` (tests, typecheck, layers, unused) is clean.
**You were right, and the release was a shortcut.** I let go of the constraints that tie a carried sketch part to something else because it was the easy way to stop the solver pulling the sketch back out of the body. They are constraints of the whole body now: not released. The solver holds the body's sketch where it is whenever it works on a constraint between it and something else (`compiler/solve.ts`), so a move that would break one is refused with the usual message and the red flash on the constraint (test: a circle's centre on the world origin cannot be moved away from it), and a move that keeps it holds. A free drawing tied to the body's sketch (a circle made concentric with it) is the thing that has to follow, and goes along with the body (test). Pinned corners (`fixed`) are not released either: they are held at their new place.
**Scale now carries the sketch too:** the sketch is scaled about the same point as the body (the middle of the kernel's box of the body, recorded in the step and used by the kernel; `Body.bbox`), its circles' radii and the sizes you locked on it (length, distance, diameter, radius) are made as many times bigger, and the extrude reads it back through the scale (`operation/frames.ts` is now a motion that keeps shapes: move, turn, same-all-ways scale; the 2D coordinates of the profile and the radius are taken back by the scale too). Test: scale by 2, then move, then turn: the body is 8 times the volume, the sketch is at its middle, the locked diameter is 32; a scale made before this change keeps its old meaning. The default turn pivot is now the kernel's box middle too (it was the middle of the corners, which a round body's corners do not reach).
**The repeats (linear, circular):** nothing to carry: they make copies and move nothing; the sketches stay with the original (copies have none).
**Not done / know:** a free drawing that is tied to the body's sketch by a constraint that cannot follow a scale (a locked distance to the body's sketch) can make the scale refused; the sketches of a body made by several extrudes on different planes are all carried (each plane once); nothing shows the sketches again (no History "sketch" button).

## Handoff 197 — 2026-11-07 (a new fillet / chamfer on one edge drawn by scaling a reference)
`in_221`. Checks clean (all four read); looked at in the browser (a box edge: dragging the double arrow rounded it live up to 3.8 mm, 6 ms a frame, the step made on release). **What:** a new fillet or chamfer cannot be made by moving what is there (the rounding does not exist yet), but its cross-section is the same shape at every size, scaled about the sharp edge. So for one **straight** edge the kernel makes the rounding once at a size (the **reference**: the first size the pointer gives; fine mesh, not the draft one) and every size after it is that result scaled on the graphics card (`frame.edgeNew`, the same `Shift` scale as for an existing rounding); the new rounding face is found as the one face the reference has and the body did not. When the size grows past 1.6 times the reference a new reference is asked for (the old one stands in until it comes, so the arc keeps enough points). Until the first reference comes (about a tenth of a second) the body is shown as it is. Fillet to chamfer (the sign) is a new reference. A size the kernel cannot make (too big for the faces) is not scaled to: the kernel's own answer (its error) is shown. The finished step is the kernel's own, the picture stays until its answer. **Still through the kernel:** a curved edge, several selected edges (and any chain), anything with a step after it.

**3. Filling a face (hover, select) cut it into triangles again for every picture**, and the cutting itself grew with the cube of the points (it started over from the first corner after each cut). The triangles of a polygon are now kept, and cutting goes round once; a 400 point concave polygon is cut in milliseconds and covers exactly its area (`test/fillholes.test.ts`).

## Handoff 226 — 2026-11-09 (the sparse solve)
`in_249` (approval of the sparse solve, and a question: how much do the last decimals matter, and sparse only while dragging?). All four checks read and clean (386 tests).
**Built:** the solver's linear algebra is sparse (`structureOf`, `placesOf`, `sparseSolve` in `compiler/solver/solver.mjs`): the matrix is kept as its non-zero entries only, eliminated in a minimum-degree order, and the points' places are looked up by name rather than by a key built each time. A move of a corner of the 135-constraint sketch: first 1400 to 2800 ms; after Handoff 225 60 to 170 ms; now **30 to 70 ms** (a 20 step drag in the browser: 30 to 40 s at first, 2.5 s, now 1.2 s of work including the picture's own).
**The decimals, measured (not guessed):** I solved 211 moves on 31 fixtures with the old (dense, bit for bit the original) solver and the sparse one and compared every corner and radius. Same status and same constraint counts every time; the positions differ in **162 of 211 moves above 1e-12**, 83 above 1e-9, 26 above 1e-8, **one above 1e-7 (1.05e-7)**; none above 1e-6. The cause is not the sparse method as such: the solver stops when every residual is under 1e-9, so a position is only fixed to about that (more where constraints nearly repeat each other: the worst was `inset-chamfer`), and any change of rounding, even a different browser's `Math.hypot`, moves the stopping place by that much. Things that look at positions use 1e-6 (a corner sharing a spot, a coincident join, the kernel's sewing), a tenth of a micron against ten times the worst difference: no decision I know of changes.
**Not two tiers:** I recommended one solver everywhere (and did that). With two (sparse while dragging, dense on release) the model on release would not be what the drag showed, only to 1e-8, but replaying a file (every command is solved again on opening) would have to use one of them: the slow one makes opening slow, the fast one makes the second tier pointless. One path, one answer.
**Test:** `test/solverspeed.test.ts` has the speed guard and the old solver's answers for two moves (`test/fixtures/laggy-moves.expected.json`), which the new solver must match to 1e-6. If someone changes the solver and the positions move by more, it fails.
**Next, if wanted:** a drag step is now about 60 ms, of which the solve is about half; the rest is working out the faces again after each move (`withDerived`: finding the regions where curves cross) and filling the face the pointer is on (400 points cut again every step, because the polygon is moving). Both could be made incremental.

(An earlier 300 seed run that seemed to hang was three runs fighting for the machine plus the one before it, not a hang: seed 44 alone takes a second.) **Found while timing the sweep: opening `hollow-pushed` takes 8 s.** It has sixteen steps, most of them moves and turns of the whole body. Each step re-runs the chain of kernel operations, and the kernel's memory of results (96 kept) is defeated once body moves have carried the sketches: every sketch, extrusion and rounding is worked out again for each new step, and every move step adds a move to the chain. Part of it was silly: a `transform` found the body's box (as slow as the move itself) even for a plain move that does not need it. **Fixed:** the box is found only when a turn or scale has no pivot (`interpreter.mjs`): 8.0 s → 5.1 s for that file and 21.5 s → 14.6 s over all 35 files; results hashed identical (faces, edges, mesh, volume) on all of them. **Left:** the re-work itself (about 5 s for 16 steps): a long history of moves costs more with every step, both when opening and after every edit. The fix is to key the kernel's memory by what the result is (the sketch as it was drawn, not as it was carried), or to fold a run of moves into one; both touch how the graph is built, so I did not do it unasked.
**Seen, not fixed:** the kernel's memory grows by about 35 MB for each opening of that file (rss 185 MB → 1.7 GB over 24 openings, in node). I tried freeing the kept results as they are let go: no change, so most of it is elsewhere (the temporary shapes of each operation, which wait for the garbage collector, which cannot see the kernel's memory). In the browser a long session could reach the tab's limit. Worth a look by itself: free the shapes an operation is done with; measure with the browser's memory graph.
**The random user, for next time:** it does not yet run with the kernel answering later (the browser's "lagging" mode), nor with files opened from the fixtures as the start. Both are the next layer.

## Handoff 230 — 2026-11-10 (the long history: a speed fix that was also a correctness fix)
`in_252` (approval to do the long-history re-work first). All four checks read and clean (390 tests).
**Why every step re-did the whole chain:** an extrusion reads its sketch back through the moves and turns that came after it (the sketch is carried with the body), to where it was drawn. Every new move changes that composition, and the sketch's frame (origin, directions) came back the same only to about 1e-14, so the kernel's memory of what it had made from that sketch never matched: the sketch, extrusion, rounding and every move before were all worked out again for each new step.
**Fixed (three things):**
1. **The frame is rounded to 1e-9** (`graph.ts`). Now the memory matches: for the 16 step file, each new step works out one new operation instead of all of them.
2. **The memory's keys are short.** A key written out was the operation plus the keys of everything it was built on, so a chain's keys grew with the square of its length (writing and comparing them was 40 % of the time left); they are now a two part 64 bit hash (`fingerprint` in `interpreter.mjs`).
3. **The description of the body (mesh, faces, edges, corners) is kept for the last six bodies** (`evaluate.mjs`), by what the body was made from: a change that leaves the body as it is (a sketch beside it, a constraint, undo and redo, the answer taken in again) no longer makes it again. A copy goes out each time (the worker hands the mesh to the page, which empties it).
**Numbers:** opening `hollow-pushed` (16 steps, 13 of them moves and turns): 8.0 s at the start of today's hunt, 2.3 s now; `rotate-slow` 1.65 s → 0.77 s; all 31 files together 21.5 s → 10.2 s. The 144-post grid and the drawing of 90 rectangles are unchanged.
**And a real bug, found on the way:** with the old numbers **a turn of the whole body changed its volume**: in `hollow-pushed`, command 28 (a rotate) took the body from 1976.081 to 1974.008 mm³, and `rotate-weird` lost 10 mm³ the same way (a rigid turn cannot change a volume). The 1e-14 of noise in the carried sketch's frame was enough to make the kernel's boolean steps (faces that meet exactly after a turn) come out differently. With the frame rounded the volume stays the same through every move and turn (checked at every step of five fixtures). This may be the cause of the "weird state after a rotate" you reported earlier (`in_230`, `in_231`); the files you sent are the fixtures `rotate-weird` and `rotate-slow`, and both now keep their volume. Test: `test/scaling.test.ts` (it fails with the old `graph.ts`: "a rotate changed the volume from 1976.08 to 1974.008").
**Results that changed:** because the inputs now differ by up to 5e-10, eight fixtures give a slightly different mesh (the mesher splits triangles a little differently) and the same body in every number that matters except the volumes above; `thin-wall` and `big-chamfer` by 6e-6 in volume and 1e-7 in a box corner. Nothing in the tests depended on them.
**Not done:** the kernel's memory growth (next, as agreed); the work per new step is now one operation plus the description of the body (about 120 ms for the 16 step file), which is the floor unless the description is made incremental.

## Handoff 231 — 2026-11-10 (the kernel's memory: measured, no leak in the browser; tools kept)
`in_253` (approval to start on the kernel memory growth). No app code changed. All four checks were read at the start of the session's last change (390 tests); this entry adds a folder of scripts and a record.
**What I found.** In node, the kernel's memory seemed to grow without end: 185 MB to 1.7 GB over 24 openings of one model, and 250 MB to 620 MB over 160 edits of the 144 post body. Looking closer, it is not a leak:
- **The first run was the broken memory of the kernel's results** (Handoff 230): the noise in the sketch's frame defeated it, so every opening made some 270 new shapes. Now the same openings reuse them (wasm memory stays at 126 MB over ten openings of the 22 face body, 105 MB over twelve openings of `three-circles`).
- **In a loop that never lets the event loop turn, nothing can free a shape.** Replicad frees a kernel shape when its JavaScript wrapper is garbage collected and the finalizer runs, and finalizers run between turns of the event loop. With a turn between edits, 240 edits of the 22 face body keep wasm at 105 MB; the page's real worker has those turns.
- **The kernel's memory is a high-water mark**: it grows by a fifth when it needs more and never shrinks, so a body that has spikes (a fillet, a boolean) leaves it at its largest need (up to 1.1 GB for the 22 face file in node), and it then stays there: it stopped growing after 15 edits and held for 15 more.
- **I tried the fixes people would try and they changed nothing:** freeing the kept results as they are let go (`.delete()`), freeing the copy each operation makes of what it works on, and a smaller memory (24, 4). The growth steps were the same to the megabyte.
- **In the real browser** (headless Chromium, the real worker, sixty edits of the first extrusion's depth, the whole browser's memory read after every ten): `hollow-pushed` 695 MB after loading, 818 MB after ten edits, **855 MB after sixty**; the 144 post body 751 MB, peak 929 MB, **841 MB after sixty**. Bounded, and the page's JavaScript heap does not move (57 MB and 72 MB).
So: no leak, and nothing to fix in the app. If a tab is ever reported as growing, the thing to look at first is the history (each undo step keeps its scene, which for a body of hundreds of faces is a couple of megabytes of mesh) and not the kernel.
**Kept:** the profiling scripts as `tools/perf/` (`hunt.cjs`, `hunt3.cjs`, `browsermem.cjs`, `cpuprofile_summary.py`, and a README with the method): they need Playwright, which is not a project dependency.
**The speed work is finished for now**, apart from what each handoff lists as left: a drag step on a big sketch (about 60 ms), the body's description after every step (about 120 ms for 16 steps), opening a file of hundreds of separate commands.

It is in now (this commit), the results are the same as without it on all 32 fixtures (hashed), and it does what Handoff 230 said (undo, redo and the kernel's answer taken in again no longer make the body's description again). The numbers in Handoff 230 (8.0 s to 2.3 s) were measured without it and stand.
**The kernel answering later, in the random user** (`laggingKernel` in `test/helpers/fuzz.ts`): the real `kernel-async.ts` (its cache and its one-at-a-time queue) with a worker that is ours to turn. A job waits until the run lets the kernel's thread finish it (how often is chosen per run: hardly ever, now and then, often), the answer goes back, and the session is made again from it exactly as `main.ts` does (`refresh`). New checks: nothing that adds a step is accepted while the answer for the last change is out; a change that was refused (`conflict.culprits`) is not in the model; when the kernel has answered everything, what is shown is no longer marked as an older model. The deep checks wait until the answers are in. Run by the normal checks (a few runs) and for longer with `FUZZ_SEEDS=200 FUZZ_STEPS=100 FUZZ_FIRST=1` (it picks the lagging runs when the test says so; `fuzz(seed, steps, undefined, { lagging: true })`).
**Sweep:** seeds 1 to 220, 60 to 70 gestures each, about 45,000 intents, about 1,600 of them with the picture marked as an older model. **Found one real bug:** the kernel **threw** (a WebAssembly exception from `evaluate`) when a cut took all of the body away: a circle bigger than the box, extruded the other way into it. The kernel asks the result for its box, an empty shape has none, and nothing caught it: in the page's worker the job failed and the step hung in "the answer is out"; with the kernel on the page's own thread (the fallback) it would have thrown out of the app. Now `evaluate` takes it as an answer: the step that emptied the body gets the error "nothing would be left of the body (the cut takes all of it away)", so it is refused (or taken back when the answer comes), like any step the kernel cannot make (`evaluate.mjs`; tests in `test/kernelempty.test.ts`).
**Checks changed, not bugs:** (1) a gesture can make several changes (one click on the line tool drew a line and then asked for ten constraints it was near, one by one) and say that a later one was refused: that is allowed, so the check is that what was refused is not in the model. (2) The two timing guards in `scaling.test.ts` failed twice when the checks ran beside other work (wall time, and then processor time, which counts the kernel's helper threads): they now compare a small size with a bigger one, measured together (a drawing of 30 rectangles with 90, which takes ten times as long because every command is derived again, and the cubic cost it had before took twenty-five; 16 posts with 144), so a busy machine slows both alike.
**Seen in the sweep, by design:** the kernel's messages in the runs with it answering later are the same as without, plus "This object has been deleted" and "upstream … failed" from steps whose earlier step was taken back, and "the kernel could not make a fillet of that size on this edge" (a fillet too big). I did not look into "This object has been deleted" (105 of 45,000 intents): it names the kernel's own wording when an operation uses a shape that was freed; it appears only while a step is waiting for its answer, and goes. If it shows in the page, that is the place to look.
**Not run yet:** the random user starting from the saved models (fixtures) instead of an empty drawing or a box.

## Handoff 233 — 2026-11-10 (what "This object has been deleted" was: two real bugs about sides shrinking to nothing)
`in_254`, continued. All four checks read and clean (396 tests). I said in Handoff 232 that I had not looked into the kernel message "This object has been deleted" (105 of 45,000 intents); I looked. It was not a kernel memory error: it is what the kernel says when it is given a **sketch with a side of no length**, and the sketch had one because of two bugs of ours:
1. **A side could be made level and upright at once.** Selecting a vertical side and choosing "Horizontal" was offered and accepted; the solver "solved" it by shrinking the side to nothing (a side that is both level and upright must have no length), the rectangle collapsed, and an extrusion made from it broke with the message above, left in the model. **Fixed twice:** the menu no longer offers it ("it is held upright already: it would have no length", and the same the other way round), and **the solver treats a side it would shrink to nothing as a conflict** (`solve.ts`: the sketch stays as it was, the relations at that side are named, and the change is refused as any conflicting change is). That also covers the other ways to get there (a side held parallel and square to the same line, and so on). Tests: `test/collapse.test.ts` (the second one fails without the solver check).
2. **A drawn figure repeated round a point copied its level and upright relations as they were.** A copy turned a quarter has them the other way round, and a copy turned by any other angle (three copies: 120 degrees) cannot keep them: the solver pulled such copies out of shape or to no length, and nothing said so. Now a quarter turn swaps level and upright (and same height and same position across), and a copy turned by any other angle gets none of those four (`pattern.ts`). Test in `test/gizmo.test.ts`: with 3, 4 and 5 copies every side of every copy keeps its length (it fails without the fix). One older test of the repeat had been passing while the copies were collapsed (it only counted faces); it passes now for the right reason.
**The sweep after these:** 150 seeds, 70 gestures each, with the kernel answering later: 29,000 intents, no failures, and the only kernel messages are the three that are by design ("select lines to extrude" and "a part of this step is gone" for an extrusion whose figure was deleted or cut, and "there is no body yet"). "This object has been deleted", "upstream ... failed" and the fillet too big message are gone from the list.
**Open:** the random user starting from the saved models (the fixtures).

`in_255`, continued. All four checks read and clean (398 tests).

The kernel did not come back from it (in the sweep, in a process that had already run other models, it ended in "RuntimeError: null function or function signature mismatch"; alone it ran for good). In the page that would have been a worker stuck for ever: every later answer would queue behind it, and the page would show "working" until reloaded.
**Two fixes:**
1. **The kernel refuses a scale that would make the body more than 100 metres long** ("that scale would make the body more than 100 metres long"), as an error on that step (`interpreter.mjs`; test in `test/kernelempty.test.ts`: the same graph is answered at once, and a scale of 3 still works).
2. **A job that takes too long is given up** (`kernel-async.ts`, limit 60 s): the worker is stopped and a new one started, the job is answered as an error on its last operation (so the step that asked is the one that is refused or taken back), the new worker is not asked anything until it says it is ready, and an answer that comes late from the old one is ignored. The failure path of a job that threw now does the same (before, its error was on an operation named "kernel", which no step has, so the step was not reported). Test with a thread that never answers (`test/kernel-async.test.ts`). **This is the general protection**: any other graph that makes the kernel loop (a huge move, a huge depth) no longer freezes the page for good; it costs the limit's wait.
**Run on the saved models so far** (33 models, plain 1 run, lagging 2 then 4 runs, about 20,000 intents): the failures were this one and two checks that were too strict (Handoff 235). A further lagging run (4 more runs each) is going; its result goes in the next entry.

So now: such a plane whose drawn corners all lie inside the box of the body being moved counts as that body's, and the carry (`transformFigure`, new optional `body`) gives the plane its owner, so the hole is moved once, with the body. **Test:** `test/holemove.test.ts` with the human's file (`test/fixtures/hole-body-move-in_348.cad.json`): moved along X, Y (small and large) and Z, the body is there, moved by the distance, with the same volume and one hole. **Not done / to say:** a file whose move was already made with the old code is not repaired; a plane with no owner that is on a face shared by two bodies is only claimed when all its drawn points are inside one body's box; the chamfer step (`s_029`) still stores a stale position (`z = 8.9`, from before the extrude was lengthened) and finds its edge by the faces' directions, which works here but is the open `dec_stable_references` matter; not looked at in a browser.

## Handoff 340 — 2026-11-14 (in_354: the fillet / chamfer preview that folded a block)
`in_354` gave the steps. **Reproduced by replaying the human's own session** (the state snapshot of `in_350` holds the project and the last 400 input events: the camera was set to what it was at the click, the fillet undone, and the events dispatched in Chromium, with the handle drags in them): at a frame where the bubble said 1.7 the block was a slanted slab. **Cause:** while a fillet / chamfer size is dragged, the picture stretches a reference made by the kernel at size R by `k = size / R` (`edgeNew`, `edgeDrag` in `frame.ts` / `operations.ts`), moving the faces that are the rounding (`roundingFaces`, `operation/roundings.ts`). Only the picture was wrong (the step is made by the kernel), which is why the applied result was right. **Not explained:** why it seemed to begin only after the height of a block was changed; the cause above does not depend on the height (my guess is that the human dragged to bigger sizes in that session, but I did not check that). **Fix:** the strip must lie within `1.2 R` of the edge's line (a fillet's or a chamfer's points are never further than R). **Tests:** `test/roundingreach.test.ts` (fillet and chamfer of 1.5, 5 and 8 on the corner of the 14 mm block: one rounding face, no wider than the size; it fails for 5 and 8 without the fix); a sweep of every straight edge of both blocks at reference sizes 4, 6, 8 and values 1 to 3 (fillet and chamfer), the stretched picture compared vertex by vertex with the kernel's result: before, many off by up to 18 mm; after, none of 288. ## Handoff 353 — 2026-11-14 (in_369: an arc that could not be trimmed)
`in_369` (files `Cannot_delete_line.json`, a touch session with the trim tool). **Reproduced headless:** trimming the first third of the arc `d/e_023.s0` was refused ("cannot be done without breaking other constraints", the culprits being the nine relations the trim itself added, so nothing visible flashed); the same arc trimmed further along, and the other arcs and lines, worked. **Root cause:** the solver holds an arc tangent to another circle or arc *at an end of the arc* (`tangentRound` in `solver.mjs`: that end lies on the line through both centres). `trim.ts` `carried` kept every tangent for the piece that is left; when the piece with the touching end was the one trimmed off, the kept arc had no end where they touch and the solver's answer was a conflict (the solver named `r_054`, `r_058`, `r_059`). **Fixed:** `carried` takes the ends of the arc that is left (`opts.ends`) and keeps a tangent to another round curve only if they touch at one of those ends (`touchesAtEnd`: the touching point is on the line through both centres, on both curves); trimArc and trimCircle pass them. Tangents to straight lines are kept as before (they hold anywhere on the arc). **Cost:** trimming off the end an arc touched another arc at drops that tangent (said nowhere: like the other relations a trim cannot carry); the sketch keeps its shape until something is moved. `trimtangent.test.ts` (fixture `trim-arc-tangent-in_369`, seven places along the arc, and kept / dropped); four of its tests fail without the change. **Also seen, not touched:** the file has duplicate relations (`r_051` / `r_029`, `r_052` / `r_028`: the same tangent and on-circle added twice by moves that drew them again); they solve, but are carried twice by a trim. It names the conflict with the accepted `dec_direct_modification` (no rebuilding from planes) and leaves the call to the human. **Nothing was changed in the code.**

## Handoff 380 — 2026-11-15 (in_403, in_404: what OCCT and the kernel are, the cost of our own; an explanation only)
`in_403`, `in_404`. Questions answered in chat, nothing built. Facts given: the kernel is everything geometric (exact solids, booleans, fillets, offsets, volume, meshing), not only validation (`isValidSolid` is one check in it); OCCT (Open CASCADE Technology, LGPL) is the one we run, as WebAssembly (`replicad-opencascadejs`, about 23 MB) in a worker behind `replicad`; our code around it is about 1500 lines (`compiler/kernel/`: `interpreter.mjs` 903 lines with 15 operations, `evaluate.mjs`, the adapter, the worker); the flow is steps → `graph.ts` operation graph → `interpreter.mjs` on OCCT shapes (cached by operation) → `evaluate.mjs` meshes and extracts faces, edges, vertices, volume → plain data → `compile.ts` makes the body parts named by position. A kernel of our own: a restricted one (planes and cylinders) is weeks to months, mostly robustness; a general one is years; an intersection-based model is simple for convex flat bodies and for evaluating a value at a point, and the difficulty returns at the boundary (surface-surface curves such as the tilted hole's, the faces and edges the user selects, names, exact dimensions).

## Handoff 382 — 2026-11-15 (in_405: option B built, and A and B checked in the browser)

(1) The first fix (`cavitiesInside`, handoff 434) is generic in the way it was meant to be: any solid with an inner shell, however made (the hollow tool, a subtract that leaves a void). (2) A hollow made **by subtract** fails differently: the kernel's chamfer returns a body with **one** shell and the void gone (271 mm3 becomes 985), which the library's check and `cavitiesInside` both pass: not caught by the first fix. (3) A cup with an open top (one shell) is caught by the library's own check. **Added:** `roundIsSane` (`interpreter.mjs`): a fillet or chamfer of `size` along edges of length `L` changes the volume by at most about `size² (L + 2 size)` (a bevel is half of `size² L`, a round less, a little more where edges meet); **L is first the selected edges and, if that does not hold the change, all the edges of the faces they bound** (the kernel carries a round on along the edges that continue the selected one, a rim: a first version with only the selected edges refused ten legitimate rounds in the suite, such as a 2.8 mm chamfer that took 377 mm3 off a 19 mm edge); more than that is not what a round makes, so the kernel's round is refused as invalid and the existing fallback (the round made as a region, `dec_edits_saturate`) makes it: the subtract-made body becomes 258.76 mm3 (271 less the wedge, less the part of the wedge that was cavity), the cavity opened. Applied to the kernel's round and to the two-distance chamfer. **Still not generic, said plainly:** both checks are about rounds and cavities; any other operation that makes a self-intersecting body the library calls valid (a hole near a thin wall, an extrude cut, a shell) is checked only by the library. **A general safety net not built:** a test of the final mesh for triangles that cross (with a bounding-volume tree; it finds any self-intersection whatever made it, and costs time on a big mesh), or an independent reference build (the round made as a region and compared); the library's `BOPAlgo_ArgumentAnalyzer` and `BRepAlgoAPI_Check`, which check self-intersection, are not in this build of it. **Asked:** whether to build the mesh test.
 **A flake noticed:** `scaling.test.ts` (the 144 posts against 16) failed once in a full run and passed alone and in the next full run: a timing ratio under load, not this change.

## Handoff 467 — 2026-11-16 (in_501: the mesh test for a body that passes through itself)
`in_501`. **Built (`dec_self_crossing_check`, accepted):** `occt/selfcross.mjs` (`findCrossing`: a sweep along the longest axis over the triangles' boxes; two triangles that share no corner and are not in one plane cross when each has corners on both sides of the other's plane by more than `depth` and the stretches of the line where the planes meet overlap by as much; `depth` = 3 x the meshing error + a ten-thousandth of the size; gives up after 3 x 10^7 pairs) and `crossesItself` in `interpreter.mjs`: the solids an operation *made* (not the ones it left as they were: copies of a shape share what they are made of, so the same solid keeps its `hashCode`: found out by a test, since a first version that meshed every solid after every operation cost 200 ms on a cube edited beside two lofts, now 1 to 2 ms) are meshed coarsely and looked at; a crossing refuses the operation (`that would make the body pass through itself`) for every operation but a plain move or turn, and inside a fillet or chamfer so that the refused round falls back to the region. Bodies of more than 600 faces are not looked at. **Verified:** with `cavitiesInside` off, the mesh test alone refuses the kernel's chamfer on the hollow of `in_499` (260.33 mm3, the right answer, from the region); with both off the bad 257.6 comes back. **Tests:** `test/selfcross.test.ts` (a cavity through the wall, inside, touching, apart; a box, a cylinder: none; the budget; separate overlapping bodies are not one crossing; a solid with a hollow through its wall is found). **Said plainly in the decision:** a crossing shallower than the meshing error (about 0.3 % of the body's size) is not seen, nor one right at a shared corner. **Not done:** the cost on a big hollow model; the 600-face limit is a guess, not measured.
 **A finding worth keeping:** meshing a solid leaves its triangles on its faces (the faces are shared by every copy of the shape), and the library's validity check then fails on a shape that was valid before (the thin-wall body of `in_229`: its fillet step was refused as `kernel returned an invalid solid`, though the mesh test found no crossing); the check now takes the triangles off again (`BRepTools.Clean`) after meshing. The mesh of the answer (`describeSolid`) is made after the operations, so it is not affected. Full suite 812 passing.
